PRELIMINARY PLAT DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and 
decisions on a proposed preliminary plat, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following 8 criteria, as found in Section 2.1.2.d:
 
1. PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in PlanCheyenne.  
 
PlanCheyenne is supportive of this proposed plat.  
 
PlanCheyenne has multiple policies which encourage 
infill development which maximizes the use of 
infrastructure investments. The property is within the 
Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
 
This property, and all the surrounding area, is 
designated as Urban Residential by the Future Land 
Use Map in PlanCheyenne. This future land use 
designation is intended to develop with a variety of 
residential types and densities; however, proposed 
development should take into consideration adjacent 
development, and the Comprehensive Plan does state 
that it is not the intent of the Plan to change existing 
stable neighborhoods. While the plat does propose lots 
that are smaller than those in the surrounding 
neighborhood, the plat is generally proposing lots of 
similar widths and the applicant has indicated Lots 2 and 
3 would likely develop with single-family homes, 
matching the pattern already found in the neighborhood.  
 
 
2. PLANNING - The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to 
existing and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities.  
 
This plat proposes creating three residential lots in the MR Medium-density Residential zone district. 
All surrounding properties are previously developed and are currently zoned MR. This platting action 
would not have an undue influence on the potential redevelopment of surrounding properties. The 
lots proposed with this platting action are significantly smaller in square footage than the surrounding 
parcels; however, the proposed lots are similar in street frontage to surrounding parcels. The impact 
of two new single-family homes would likely be minor, as the new development would have similar 
front and side setbacks to the existing homes in the neighborhood. 
 
As Hilltop Avenue is designated as a collector road, access to Lots 2 and 3 would be limited. Lot 2 
would be required to access off of the alley, and Lot 3 would be required to access off of Bluff Place. 
Exact access location would be addressed at the time of building permit.  
Map #3 – Future Land Use  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Expedited Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00021 
7 
 
 
 
 
3. SUBDIVISION STANDARDS - Division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in 
Article 4. 
 
Transportation Network: 
The plat proposes dividing land within an established street network – no additions or 
subtractions from the transportation network are suggested. This plat is part of a block which has 
a perimeter of about 5,300 linear feet. The standard for maximum perimeter for newly 
developing residential areas is 2,200 linear feet. However, there is no opportunity for this plat to 
create a meaningful break in the existing block, and Staff’s judgement is that the plat qualifies for 
the infill exception from maximum block sizes (UDC 4.3.3.c.6). 
 
Community Facility Fees: 
This land was platted in 1959 as part of Buffalo Ridge Estates. No City plat fees were collected 
with the original platting action. This plat proposes platting the subject property for residential 
development. The Residential Enhancement Fee is $400 per unit. For fees based on acreage, the 
area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. The required fees for the 0.5 acres 
of land in this plat are as follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
$175 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
$175 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees:  $350.00 
Buffalo Ridge Estates, 11th Filing Final Plat Map. 

Expedited Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00021 
8 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Projected Residential Enhancement Fee 
$1,200 ($400 per unit) 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. A residential structure already exists on Lot 1, therefore 
the enhancement fee for Lot 1 would only be due if the existing house was demolished and a 
new structure was built. 
 
Total Fees due at signing of the plat: $350.00 
 
Open Space: 
During the plat process, this property is not required to provide open space. Residential open 
space is not required at time of platting on infill development of less than 10 acres, as long as 
alternative open space options are available (UDC 4.4.3.b.3). 
 
Required Improvements: 
This division can provide all required utilities to the properties within this development. The 
existing structure is serviced by utilities to the north and south, and no re-alignment of utility 
service lines or easements for service lines are necessary. 
 
 
4. PHASING - Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public facilities of the 
subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the likelihood of future phases being 
constructed as planned. 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. The existing structure is proposed to remain on Lot 
1 – construction on Lots 2 and 3 would occur after the platting action is complete. 
 
 
5. STUDIES - The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or 
requested to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the application, can 
mitigate these impacts. 
 
The applicant submitted traffic and drainage worksheets. Due to the size of the property, the 
stormwater water quality requirements will need to be met at the time of any construction. No other 
studies were requested with this application and no further concerns were identified by Staff. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

Expedited Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00021 
9 
 
6. ZONING - All proposed lots shall be buildable lots 
according to the existing or proposed zoning district 
standards, and result in site design and development 
patterns that meet the Intent and Applicability statements of 
the existing or proposed zoning district. All parcels not 
proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to 
and accepted by the City shall have a clearly defined entity 
responsible for ongoing maintenance. 
 
The proposed lot sizes are within the range allowed in 
the MR zone. The new lot 1 has the frontage and area 
to be considered the DD5 lot type. The new lots 2 and 3 
have the frontage and area to be considered the DD7 or 
SD1 lot type. The MR zone in which this property is 
located “is intended for a mix of density and dwelling 
types within a neighborhood [and] applicable to areas 
where more compact neighborhood development 
patterns are desired.” (ref UDC 5.3.2) The district’s 
regulations are designed to encourage these uses, while 
also protecting nearby areas. The proposed plat meets 
these objectives.  
 
 
7. SURROUNDING DEVELOPMENT - The application will not create any undue influence on existing or 
potential development for adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan 
policies for that property. 
 
The typical single-family lots east of Hilltop (near the proposed plat) are about 75’ wide and 
12,000sqft. The typical single-family lots west of Hilltop (near the proposed plat) are about 70’ wide 
and 9,000sqft. Lot 1 is proposed at about 83’ wide and 12,000sqft. Lot 2 is proposed at about 83’ 
and 6,000sqft. Lot 3 is proposed at about 69’ wide and 6,000sqft. The size of Lots 2 and 3 are 
significantly smaller than the typical lots in the area, but the frontage of all three lots is similar to the 
surrounding neighborhood. Front setbacks would remain similar throughout the neighborhood and 
the lack of depth to the proposed parcels should not unduly influence surrounding properties. 
 
 
8. PUBLIC HEALTH, SAFETY, AND WELFARE – Whether the application will harm the public health, safety or 
general welfare. 
 
Staff is satisfied the proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, or general 
welfare.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Marian Orr and City Council 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
February 19, 2020  
SUBJECT: 
Revolving Real Property Fund 
 
 
On February 24, 2020, the Governing Body will be referring a resolution to the Finance Committee 
for consideration of creating a Revolving Real Property Fund.   This would be a Special Revenue 
Fund similar to the City of Casper’s Revolving Land Fund.   
The purpose of the Revolving Real Property Fund would be to create a mechanism to facilitate the 
City of Cheyenne’s acquisition and resale of real property.  The Governing Body would decide on 
a case-by-case basis if the proceeds of any real property sale should be deposited into this new 
fund.  The proceeds would earn interest and be used for future real property capital expenditures, 
instead of being absorbed into the General Fund and used for operating expenditures as was done 
in the past.   
This new fund would allow the City to be fiscally responsible by saving for the construction or 
acquisition of much needed future capital expenditures such as a new fire station, a gymnasium, 
or even remodeling or reconstructing the Municipal Building. The funds could also be used to pay 
for architectural and engineering related design services for future 6th penny ballot projects. 
Please feel free to call me at (307) 637-6336 if you have questions.  Thank you for your 
consideration. 
 
 



--- END OF FILE ---

Tenant, nor by the voluntary or involuntary liquidation, sale, or other disposition of all or substantially all
of the assets ofTenant, or by the release of any other guarantor. Landlord and Tenant, without notice to or
consent by Guarantor, may at any time or times enter into such modifications, extensions, amendments or
other covenants respecting the Lease as they may deem appropriate, and Guarantor shall not be released
thereby but shall continue to be fully liable to the performance of all obligations and duties of Tenant under
the Lease as so modified, extended or amended.
Guarantor further agrees (1) to indemnify and hold harmless Landlord from and against any claims,
damages, expenses, or losses, including to the extent permitted by law, the reasonable fees of an attorney
(including in-house and staff counsel), resulting from or arising out of any breach of the Lease by Tenant
or by reason of Tenant’s failure to perform any of its obligations thereunder, and (2) to the extent permitted
by law, to pay any costs or expenses, including the reasonable fees of an attorney (including in-house and
staff counsel). incurred by Landlord in enforcing this Guaranty.
The Guarantor acknowledges that Landlord may assign its rights under the Lease to an institutional
investor as security for a loan to be made by such institutional investor to Landlord, and as long as any
indebtedness of Landlord shall be outstanding and such assignment of the Lease shall exist, such
institutional investor assignee shall be entitled to bring any suit, action or proceeding against the
undersigned for the enforcement of any provision ofthis Guaranty. and it shall not be necessary in any such
5

Suit, action or proceeding to make Landlord a party thereto. This Guaranty may not be modified or amended
without the prior written consent of such assignee of Landlord’s interest in the Lease, and any attempted
modification or amendment without such Consent shall be void.
All existing and future advances by Guarantor to Tenant and all existing and future debts of Tenant
to any Guarantor shall be subordinated to all obligations owed to Landlord under the Lease and this
Guaranty. Guarantor assumes the responsibility to remain informed of the financial condition of Tenant
and of all other circumstances bearing upon the risk of Tenant’s default, which reasonable inquiry would
reveal, and agrees that Landlord shall have no duty to advise Guarantor ofinformation known to it regarding
such condition or any such circumstance.
Landlord shall not be required to inquire into the powers of
Tenant or the officers, employees, partners or agents acting or purporting to act on its behalf, and any
indebtedness made or created in reliance upon the professed exercise of such powers shall be guaranteed
tinder this Guaranty. Each Guarantor hereby represents and warrants to Landlord that such Guarantor has
received a copy of the Lease, has read or had the opportunity to read the Lease, and understands the terms
of the Lease.
The provisions in the Lease relating to the execution of additional documents, legal
proceedings by Landlord against Tenant, severability of the provisions of the Lease, interpretation of the
Lease, notices, waivers, the applicable laws which govern the interpretation of the Lease and the authority
of Tenant to execute the Lease are incorporated herein in their entirety by this reference and made a part
thereof. Any reference in those provisions to “Tenant” shall mean each Guarantor and any reference in
those provisions to the “Lease” shall mean this Guaranty.
If any one or more of the provisions of this Guaranty shall be held to be invalid, illegal or
unenforceable in any respect, such invalidity, illegality or unenforceability shall not affect any other
provision of this Guaranty, and this Guaranty shall be construed as ifsuch invalid, illegal or unenforceable
provision had never been contained herein. This Guaranty shall be construed according to the laws of the
state where the Leased Premises are located (the “Statet’). By execution hereof, the undersigned specifically
consent to this choice of law designation and consent that all actions or proceedings arising directly,
indirectly or otherwise in connection with, out of, related to, or from this Guaranty or the Lease shall be
litigated only in the courts located in the State, and the undersigned (i) consent and submit to the in
persona jurisdiction of any state or federal court located within the State, (ii) waive any right to transfer
or change the venue of litigation brought against the undersigned, and (iii) agree to service of process, to
the extent permitted by law, by mail.

ON THE LEASE.

Interpretation. Both parties have rend this Lease and had the opportunity to employ legal counsel and negotiate

21.19
Lessee represents and warrants to Lessor that no broker or agent negotiated or was instrumental in

All fees, commissions or

ARTICLE XXII:
SECURITY AND RENT DEPOSITS
22.01
Amount of Security Deposit.
Lessee, contemporaneously with the execution of this Lease, has deposited with
Lessor the sum set forth in Section 1.01 (m), the receipt of which is hereby acknowledged by Lessor.
Said
deposit shall be held by Lessor, wtihout liability for interest, as security for the faithful performance by Lessee of
all the terms, covenants and conditions of this Lease by said Lessee to be kept and performed during the term
hereof. Lessee specifically agrees that any deposit held hereunder by Lessor may be commingled with any other
funds of Lessor.
22.02
Use and Return of Security Deposit.
Should Lessee fail to keep and perform any of the terms, covenants and
conditions of this Lease to be kept and performed by Lessee, as provided in Article XVI, Lessor may appropriate
and apply said entire deposit, or so much thereof as may be necessary, to compensate Lessor for loss or damage
sustained by Lessor due to such breach, without prejudice to its further rights and remedies.
Should the entire
security deposit or any portion thereof he appropriated and applied by Lessor for the payment of overdue rent or
other sums due from Lessee hereunder, the Lessee shall, upon the written demand of Lessor, forthwith remit to
Lessor a sufficient amount in cash to restore said deposit to the original sum deposited.
Should Lessee comply
with all the terms, covenants and conditions of’ this Lease, the said deposit shall be returned in full to Lessee at
the end of the term of this Lease or upon its earlier termination,
22.03
Rent Dpoait.
ARTICLE XXIII:
LESSEE COVENANT5 EASEMENTS
23.01
Lessee Covenants.
Notwithstanding anything to the contrary contained herein, this Lease is subject to and mK1e
on the understanding that Lessor has granted and/or will grant certain restrictions and exclusive use covenants to
other Lessees of the Shopping Center (herein the “Lessee Covenants”).
Lessee acknowledges that Lessee’s use
and/or occupancy of the leased premises in violation of any current or future Lessee Covenants would subject
Lessor to substantial damages, and as such, Lessee acknowledges and agrees that any such violation by Lessee of
any such Lessee Covenants shall constitute a default hereunder entitling Lessor to cancel this Lease or enjoin
Lessee from violating such Lessee Covenants, or exercise any of the remedies stated in Article XVI hereof and
any other remedies available under the law of the State.
Nothing contained in this Section 23.01
shall be
construed to permit Lessee to expand the use restrictions set forth in Section 4.01 hereof.
23.02
Easements.
The Shopping Center is and/or may be encumbered and/or benefited from time to time by certain
easements, development and operating cOvenants, and similar agreements.
Lessee agrees that it shall abide by
any such agreement, including as any such agreement may be amended from time to time in Lessor’s sole
discretion.
Lessor shall have the right to enter into and/or terminate any such agreement in Lessor’s sole
discretion.
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, Lessor and Lessee have executed this Lease as of the day and year first above written.

01/13/2020
Dear Marian Orr and Cheyenne City Council members,
My name is Ai Qin Zhu.
I would like to introduce myself to you as the new owner of C & 1 LLC dba China
Buffet.
China Buffet has been a staple in the community of Cheyenne since 1998 and we will continue to uphold
this tradition. Although we are new owners, we have managed or have been associated with China
Buffet for the better half of a decade. Our plan is to continue with our current staff and operations
which will allow us to continue to serve the people of Cheyenne, and its many visitors, the amazing
traditional Chinese food we have all come to expect. We are very excited to have ownership in such a
long standing establishment and look forward to working with the community to keep Cheyenne the
beautiful, charming city it is.
We appreciate your time and patronage.
Sincerely,
Ai Qin Zhu

W.S. 6-5-308. Penalty for filing false document.
(a) A person commits a felony punishable by imprisonment for not more than two (2) years, a fine

and willfully or knowingly:
(i) Falsifies, conceals or covers up by any trick, scheme or device a material fact;
(ii) Makes any materially false, fictitious or fraudulent statement or representation; or

false, fictitious or fraudulent statement or entry.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2020-02-04_TotalExtraction_49489238.pdf
  
 
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
February 24, 2020 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete Laybourn (arrived at 6:03 p.m.), Mike 
Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also present: Kylie Soden, 
Deputy City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Mr. White, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Cook and Mr. Case, agenda item #18 (a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. 
Luna moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on February 10, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from the Special Meeting of the Governing Body on February 11, 2020.  Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for a microbrewery liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Black Tooth Brewing Company, LLC d/b/a/ Black Tooth Brewing, 520 
W. 19th St., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  There being no comments or input and purpose of the hearing 
having been met, the item was referred to the Finance Committee and Mayor Orr closed the 
hearing. 
 
Mayor Orr opened a public hearing for renewal of existing City liquor licenses (retail, 
restaurant, limited retail, resort, bar and grill licenses, microbrewery, satellite distillery, winery 
and special malt beverage permits) filed with the City Clerk’s Office.  There being no comments 
or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, the item was referred to the Finance 
Committee and Mayor Orr closed the hearing. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 5.52, Massage Establishments, 
within Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  Rachael Wetzel commented on the importance of the ordinance for women in the 
community, the need for the availability of breast massage services within city limits where there 
are regulations and spoke in support of the ordinance.  Mayor Orr expressed her appreciation to 

  
Ms. Wetzel for her involvement and for bringing the issue forward for consideration.  City 
Attorney Michael O’Donnell advised he has been in contact with the Director of the Environmental 
Health Department related to the requirement of a medical referral for breast massage service 
patients and that they endorse the ordinance.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. Case moved to amend by 
substitute dated February 24, 2020 which includes striking language “or distance learning school” 
in Section 5.52.105 (a)(2) and Section 5.52.105 (b)(2), seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion to amend 
by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main motion as amended 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapters 8.40, Food and Food Service 
Establishments, within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, and Chapters 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking Generally, and 10.68, Critical 
Traffic and Parking Areas, within Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapters 10.12, Administration and 
Enforcement, 10.16, Arrest and Citation Procedure, 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking 
Generally, 10.60, Parking Limitations, 10.64, Loading and Unloading, within Title 10, Vehicles 
and Traffic, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Case moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Amending Section 10.12.130, City Traffic Engineer, of 
Chapter 10.12, Administration and Enforcement, of Title 10, Vehicles and Traffic, of the 
Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Public Services Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. Case) 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Creating Section 2.04.200, Purchasing Policy, of Chapter 
2.04, Mayor and City Council, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the Municipal Code of 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Case)  
 
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Alliance 
Addition, 4th Filing, a replat of a portion of Lot 1, Block 1, Alliance Addition, Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of the intersection of Ridge Road and Jackson 
Street.  Mr. Case moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Luna. Comment was made on the proposed 
land use worsening existing parking issues in the area.  Motion failed. Voting “yes” – Mr. Case, 
Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Luna, 
Mayor Orr, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Roybal. 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for Buffalo 
Ridge Estates, 11th Filing, a replat of Lot 15, Block 21, Buffalo Ridge Estates, Cheyenne, Laramie 
County, Wyoming (located southeast of the intersection of Hilltop Avenue and Bluff Place).  Mr. 
Case moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  Concern was expressed related to the lot size and 

  
the proposal of dividing the lot into 2 residential lots.  Motion failed due to a tie vote.  Voting 
“yes” – Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. 
Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Luna, Mayor Orr and Dr. Rinne. 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Creating a Revolving Real Property Special Revenue Fund to account 
for the acquisition and resale of real property of the City of Cheyenne – referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the City of Cheyenne to approve and file the Wyoming 
Division of Victim Services Funding Application State Fiscal Year 2021 and 2022, July 1, 2020 
to June 30, 2022 – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the Fiscal Year 2020 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §16-4-112 & §16-4-113 – referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsors – Mr. Case, Mr. Luna, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Shanor) 
 
 
Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Blue Federal Credit Union, 
Inc., for the redevelopment and access at the Pershing Boulevard/Converse Avenue/19th Street 
Roundabout.  City Engineer Tom Cobb explained the agreement is for possible operational 
improvements and public access in perpetuity for the free right turn and north sidewalk.  Mr. White 
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute Exhibits A, 
B and C that were distributed electronically on February 21, 2020 seconded by Mr. Luna.  Motion 
to amend by substitute exhibits carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Main 
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#7149) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Audiomatrix Inc., for 
the City of Cheyenne Public, Educational, and Governmental (PEG) Access Channel 
Programming Equipment and Production Equipment Upgrade Project (2015-2018 1% Sales Tax, 
Charter Equipment Funding Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to 
exceed $74,537.65).  (#7150) 
 
[CA] 
Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County School District Number 
One to provide transportation services for the Community Recreation & Events Summer Rec 
Camp Program (Recreation Program Fees) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-11-20 for furnishing one (1) new 2019-2020 ¾ ton regular cab, 
full size pickup truck and one (1) new 2019-2020 ¾ ton ext. cab, full size pickup truck for the City 
of Cheyenne, Parks Division (2019-2022 1% Sales Tax).   Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried 
(accepted bid from Spradley Barr Motors, Cheyenne, in an amount not to exceed $67,703.00). 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-6-20 for furnishing a minimum of three (3) to a maximum of ten 
(10), lift-equipped transit vehicles, for the City of Cheyenne, Transit Division.  (FTA Transit 
Development Grant) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 

  
[CA] 
Restaurant liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for C & Z LLC, 
d/b/a China Buffet, 1958 Dell Range Blvd., Suite D, Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried.  
 
 
Voucher Report.  No comments were made. 
 
 
Revenue Report.  No comments were made. 
 
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held March 9, 2020 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O'Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a retail liquor license 
renewal application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Deep South LLC d/b/a Redwood Lounge, 
2105 E. Lincolnway, Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
 
Under other business:  
 
 
Downtown Development Authority Executive Director Amber Ash made comments on 
agenda item #9 which included the importance of food trucks in the community and concern for 
food trucks being able to park in front of businesses for long periods of time.  Ted Miazga, Parking 
Manager, Cheyenne Police Department, clarified the ordinance is to establish the option of food 
trucks being able to stay longer than 2 hours in front of a business if they are requested to be there. 
 
 
Police Chief Brian Kozak provided an update on Calea accreditation the Police Department 
is looking to obtain.  He explained it is a 3-year process and he is seeking governing body input 
before it is finalized.  Upon inquiry, Chief Kozak provided information on the accreditation 
program stating law enforcement has 181 different standards, many of which are high liability, 
and the program is to improve the delivery of public safety services and best practices.  He 
explained the department is seeking 1st level accreditation and the Wyoming Association of Risk 
Management will pay for the membership.  Upon further inquiry, Chief Kozak advised the 
accreditation may prove helpful when applying for grant funding.  Mr. Laybourn moved to approve 
and appreciate the Police Department’s accreditation application.  Concern was expressed related 
to approval of an item that hasn’t been previously reviewed.  Mayor Orr advised Chief Kozak sent 
an email with accreditation information and it can be re-sent to council members.  Motion failed 
due to lack of a second.  [Clerk’s note:  items that have not been reviewed by a standing committee 
are not eligible for consideration of approval by the governing body]    
 
 
Mr. White reported the motion to adopt the following ordinance - Pursuant to Section 2.2.1 
Zoning Map Amendments, Section 5.1.2, Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official 
Zoning Map of the Unified Development Code, changing the zoning classification from LR Low 
Density Residential to NR-2 Neighborhood Residential - Medium Density for the west half of Lot 
2 and all of Lot 3, Sunnyside Addition, 8th Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the southeast 
corner of Dell Range Blvd. and Van Buren Ave.) failed on 3rd and final reading at the February 
10, 2020 City Council meeting.  Having voted on the prevailing side and pursuant to Chapter 2.04 
of City Code, Mr. White moved to reconsider the vote taken on the ordinance, seconded by Mr. 
Cook.  Mr. White advised he reviewed additional information involving the ordinance related to 
the required notification process and requested support for the motion to reconsider.  Motion failed 

  
due to a tie vote.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Cook, Mayor Orr, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  
Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna and Dr. Rinne. 
 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:28 p.m. 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kylie Soden, Deputy City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
March 3, 2020 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2019-08-26_TotalExtraction_29950473.pdf
  
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
August 12, 2019 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were:  MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL 
MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Pete Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott 
Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White. Absent: Ken Esquibel.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  
The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Orr and Kris Jones, City Clerk, explained 
that due to a clerical error, agenda items #18 and #19 were inadvertently referred to the Finance 
Committee and are instead referred to the Public Services Committee.  Mr. Luna moved to 
approve, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body present.    
 
 [CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on July 22, 2019.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mr. Luna moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Case.   
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6, 1.1.7, and 2.4.1 of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code (UDC), amending Section 6.5.4.c to modify allowances for 
Electronic Message Centers.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Cook and Dr. Rinne voting 
“no”. (#4280) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING –  Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 Planned Development - 
Regulating Plan or Development Master Plan, Section 5.1.3 Official Zoning Map, and Section 
5.6.2 PUD – Planned Unit Development District of the Unified Development Code, amending 
Ordinance No. 2975 and Ordinance No. 3297 by modifying certain standards of the adopted 
district, Planned Unit Development (PUD), for all of Block 596 and the westerly 40 feet of the 
vacated right of way of House Avenue between East 7th and 8th Streets, Original City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming (located east of I-180 between 7th and 8th Streets).  Dr. Rinne moved to 
approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present. (#4281)  
 

  
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Vacating all portions of non-vacated: alley, adjoining 
half-street right of way of 12th Street, and adjoining half-street right of way of Holmes Avenue; 
of Block 168, Interior Heights, 2nd Filing; and all of Center Drive west of the west line of Harmony 
Center 2nd Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming (located north of and adjacent to 
West College Drive, west of Walterscheid Boulevard).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and 
final reading, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#4282) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Pursuant to Sections 1.1.6 and 2.4.1 of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (UDC), amending Section 1.4.4 and 5.1.4 of the UDC to clarify 
definitions for entertainment and recreation land uses; to create a new entertainment and recreation 
– Other Land Use; and to allow these uses in various zoning districts as administrative, conditional 
or principal permitted uses.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by 
Mr. Case. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#4283) 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the three 
hundred foot (300’) wide right-of-way of U.S. Highway No. 30, situated in a portion of Sections 
26, 27 and 34, Township 14 North, Range 66 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, 
(located between Cleveland Avenue and Hayes Avenue).  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third 
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Shanor.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present. (#4284)   
 
           ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Chapter 8.64, Smoking in Public Places, 
within Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Municipal Code for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
Amy Spieker, Cheyenne Regional Medical Center, provided statistical information relating to the 
harmful effects of smoking and supported smoke free indoor environments in public places.  Olivia 
Hardy, Jr. High student, reported increased usage of vaping devices with underage students and 
voiced support for banning use in public places.  Responding to Mayor Orr, Ms. Hardy reported 
she would support changing the age a person is legally able to purchase vaping or smoking 
products to 21 years of age.  Regina Hughes and Amanda Rieke, Straight Line Vapors, voiced 
support for stricter enforcement relating to underage smoking/vaping violators and provided 
information relating to the positives of vaping which assist smokers to quit and air quality. They 
further commented on the positive effects vaping has had on regular cigarette smokers supporting 
them to quit.  Brittney Wardle, Cheyenne Regional Medical Center, reported studies show that 
vaping devices emit chemicals into the air exposing non-users which has been associated to 
potential increases in asthma diagnosis.  David Allen, Tobacco Compliance Officer, Cheyenne 
Police Department, advised the department supports the proposed amendment.  Jason Mintzer, 
American Cancer Society Director, reported studies show vaping devices can include up to 31 
ingredients that produce/contain chemicals (some potential cancer causing that are emitted into the 
air whether or not they contain nicotine).  Mr. Mintzer requested consideration to include in the 
proposed amendment: 1-Strengthen the definition of Electronic Smoking Device, 2-Include hotels 
and motels, 3-Include private residences which are licensed child care, adult day care, pre-schools, 
or health facilities and stated he has started a petition supporting those changes.  Upon inquiry, 
Mr. Mintzer confirmed he would support enforcement when the device is being “carried” and 
lighted or heated.  Mike Morrison, American Heart Association, voiced support and commented 

  
on exposure to secondhand inhalation.   Marguerite Herman voiced support, reported increased 
use among high school users, and reiterated the addictiveness of nicotine.  Krysta Reeve, Straight 
Line Vapors, provided information regarding requiring product labeling and voiced opposition to 
the amendment.  Cheyenne Kise voiced opposition stating vaping products assisted her father in 
quitting nicotine products.  Baily Early, Jr. High student, advised that changing the age to 21 years 
would not necessarily keep youth from obtaining the devices as older friends and siblings purchase 
and provide them to minors.  Dr. Phyllis Sherrard, Cheyenne Regional Medical Center, provided 
information regarding the prevention needs assessment for Laramie County and advised teen/youth 
vaping device use has increased by approximately 32%.  Patty Reisland, Mayor’s Council for 
People with Disabilities, voiced support for the ordinance.  Nick Vaugh, Poder Academy 
Secondary School Principle, reported nicotine addiction involving vaping devices is on the 
increase among youth at the school.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Case. Mr. White moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Case.  Comment was made 
related to medical necessity of prescription inhalers.  Motion to amend carried.   Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Shanor and Mayor Orr voting 
“no”.   Further comments were made relating to expressing caution in banning legal substances 
and infringing on rights and lack of substantiated health risks regarding harmful effects of 
electronic device usage.  Mr. White explained the purposed of the amendment is to include vaping 
devices, noting they were not in existence when the original smoking ban was approved.  
Discussion topics included:  Ordinance language, enforcement, secondhand risk health reduction 
and nicotine addiction.  Main motion as amended carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Shanor and Mayor Orr voting “no”. 
 
ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, the south 
one hundred fifty feet (150’) of Tract 119, all of Tract 120, and those portions of Tracts 135 and 
136 north of the right-of-way for U.S. Hwy. No. 30, and all of vacated Cheyenne Street (60’), and 
the west ½ (25’) of vacated Fillmore Ave., adjacent to said tracts,  Sunnyside Addition Fourth 
Filing, situate in a portion of the SE1/4 SE1/4 of Section 27, Township 14N, Range 66W of the 6th 
P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming.  (located south of the intersection of Fillmore Avenue and

ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Pursuant to Section 2.2.2 - Planned Development

ORDINANCE – 1st READING – Submitting to the qualified property owners of real 
property within the boundaries of the Cheyenne Downtown Development Authority District of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, excluding real property used exclusively for residential  purposes, at a 
Special Election on Wednesday, October 16, 2019, the following Question:  “Shall a Special

RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a Final Plat for 
Harmony Valley, First Filing, a plat of a portion of the S1/2 of Section 7 and the SE1/4 NE1/4 of 
Section 7, T.13N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, (located north of and 
adjacent to W. College Drive, west of Walterscheid Boulevard).  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend by substitute and revised map dated August

governing body present.  Main motion as amended carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of the

[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the fiscal year 2019 budget of the City of Cheyenne

           Announcement of a Public Hearing to be held August 26, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in City Council 
Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a proposed trade of real property 
owned by the  City of Cheyenne, described as Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 1st 
Filing, and Lot 1, Block 1, Clear Creek Subdivision, 2nd Filing, Laramie County, Wyoming, 
Consisting of 419.57 acres and having an appraised value of $1,004,000.00, to Mariah, LLC, for 
real property owned by Mariah, LLC, described as all of Sections 11 and 14, lying north of the 
Union Pacific Railroad right-of-way, Township 12 North, Range 70 West of the 6th P.M., Laramie 
County, Wyoming, consisting of 543.76 acres and having an appraised value of $1,060,000.00.   
 
           Announcement of a Public Hearing to be held August 26, 2019 at 6:00 p.m. in City Council 
Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, for a proposed sale of undeveloped public 
property described as a 1.46 acre portion of Lot 1, Block 1, Cheyenne Ice & Events Center, 1st 
Filing, City of Cheyenne, Laramie County, Wyoming, with an appraised value of $293,000.00 to 
John J. Koehler.   
 
Under other business: 
 
Joe Dougherty commented on release of retainage to Reiman Corp. involving the Civic 
Center Commons Project with respect to cracking concrete and requested an update regarding a 
time frame for when the contractor would be repairing or replacing deficient concrete.   Mayor Orr 
advised the contractor was also selected for the Christensen Overpass Project which they are 
currently under construction.  Amy Allen, City Engineer, reported there is a punch list of items 
they will be returning to repair and advised they are currently working on coordinating that time 
frame.  Mr. Dougherty encouraged the governing body to follow up on completion of the 
contractor’s punch list.   
 
Mr. Laybourn voiced concern relating to failing/cracking concrete throughout the city 
involving city streets and the Wyoming Department of Transportation in relation to state highways 
running through and around the city, use of tax payer funding for these projects and encouraged 
members of the governing body to set standards for the future to hold contractors accountable.    
 
 
Mr. Case inquired as to the process for street sweeping.  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works 
Director, advised currently the department is understaffed and reported difficulty in recruiting 
employees due to limitations on the amount of wages that can be offered.  Ms. Nemecek provided 
a summary of the current process of street sweeping relating to commercial and residential areas, 
noting members of the public are welcome to make requests for services that would be placed on 
a waiting list.        
 

  
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:16 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk
